ParlaMint-PL_2017-09-28-sejm-48-2.u17	Thank you very much, Marshal. Mr. Marshal! Minister! According to information from the Minister of Culture and National Heritage, Poland lost about 516 thousand as a result of World War II. works of art. In June this year. The Law on the Restitution of National Cultural Goods has entered into force, which provides for a more effective and faster recovery of unlawfully lost goods. The Act also implements the European Union Directive concerning the return of cultural objects unlawfully removed from the territory of a Member State of the European Union into Polish legislation. In practice, it means faster and more efficient recovery of cultural goods lost by Poland, and it is both about those which are obvious, i.e. monuments registered in Poland, as well as about the better possibility of recovering archival materials, archives, museums that are included in inventory of various museums, and which are not monuments, as well as library materials, i.e. books that are missing or may in the future disappear from Polish collections from the national library stock. Minister, I have a question: What barriers must the Polish state still struggle with in order to recover lost cultural goods? The second question will be asked by a colleague.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-09-28-sejm-48-2.u19	Thank you. Please respond to Minister Jarosław Sellin.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-09-28-sejm-48-2.u20	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Dear Members, Thank you very much for these questions, and the subject is really important, interesting and we have recently received another instrument for effective action in this area. First of all, it should be pointed out that the Law on the Restitution of National Cultural Goods will facilitate the recovery of Polish national cultural goods located in the territories of other Member States of the European Union, because it regulates this precisely because it introduces into the Polish legal order a special procedure for judicial proceedings provided for in the 2014 Directive, which is the procedure for the return of the cultural property in the territory of the Member State from which it was illegally removed. This procedure shall apply between Member States. This procedure, which is a particular type of restitution of cultural goods, shall be carried out before a court of the State in whose territory the cultural good has been brought out, at the request of the Member State seeking reimbursement. The other party to this proceeding is the current holder of the cultural object. This procedure may be applied to cultural goods classified by the State from which they were removed as national cultural goods, provided that the export of such goods is restricted by the legislation of that State. The return proceedings shall take place before the competent courts of the State from which the refund is to be made, and the parties to the proceedings shall be the Member State requesting the refund and, on the other hand, the current holder or owner of the cultural object. The Act, in accordance with the content of that Directive, establishes the primacy of heritage [[Game in the O.R., bell ringing.]] a cultural Member State over the rights of the owner of the cultural object. This is therefore generally a strengthening of the position of the country from which the cultural good was illegally removed or stolen in relation to the existing legal situation. In addition, by introducing clear standards of competence for bodies in restitution matters, the law eliminates the dispute of competence between the Minister of Culture and National Heritage and the Minister of Foreign Affairs. So far it has been so for many years that there have been cells dealing with such matters both at the Ministry of Culture and at the Ministry of Foreign Affairs, and to be honest, sometimes we have walked into a parade, saying so a little colloquially, but that's actually what happened. Sometimes even in both resorts it was not known that they were doing exactly the same thing, they started the procedure for the same work and there was just some chaos. Now it's all in order, it's all in the Ministry of Culture and National Heritage. In addition to the above, the Act introduces solutions to counter the trafficking of cultural goods from illegal sources. This purpose is intended to introduce the obligation to keep records of cultural goods accepted or offered for disposal by economic operators specialized in the trading of monuments in the territory of the Republic of Poland. This regulation reduces the risk of abuse, violations of the law when these entities issue expertise to demonstrate that the monument is not subject to export restrictions. To answer the second question about the barriers that the Polish state still has to deal with, I would like to say that of course there are many problems, they are diverse in nature. In the first place, barriers should be identified within the legislation of the individual countries where the work of art has been found. Often works of art have passed from hand to hand since the war or have been subject to trade, which in the legal order of many countries makes it impossible to effectively rehabilitate objects on a civilian road. There is, of course, a problem of acquisition in good faith by a particular citizen who has no idea that a given work is simply stolen, that it is actually a crime. Political issues, such as the temperature of intergovernmental relations, are often essential for the recovery of works of art, especially from public collections. This applies especially to countries outside the European Union. Another problem related to the restitution process is also the lack of sources that allow unambiguous proof of the identity of the work with the Polish war loss and the demonstration of the Polish proweniety of the object. Unfortunately, many works of art were not catalogued before the war, and the existing documentation was lost as a result of war or robbery. So that here we very often rely on evidence of witnesses or photographs, which are often imperfect, because before the war they were generally black and white, and can not always be treated as 100 percent proof that the work actually originated in Poland. Russia is a separate problem. Russia has introduced special legislation which assumes that it will not return any cultural works that come from the pre-war territory of the Third Reich, i.e. this includes Polish Silesia, Pomerania, Warmia and Mazury today. Russia also interprets the question of Gdansk, which was not the territory of the Third Reich, but according to Russian legislation was. We have a problem with this in our relations with Russia. When it comes to the question of which institutions we cooperate with, of course, it is by necessity the Ministry of Foreign Affairs, because if we find a work somewhere at auction, if it is to be sold there, we immediately launch diplomatic institutions, embassies, consulates, so that they intervene, so that they simply make security quick. The point is that we can secure such a work so that it is not sold. We work with the police, of course, with the Border Guard, but above all with the facilities. The main state office, which seeks and recovers cultural works, especially those stolen during World War II, is the Department of Cultural Heritage abroad at the Ministry of Culture. [[♪ The bell ♪]] That's what it's called: the Department of Cultural Heritage overseas and the Strat Wars. A special part of this department deals with war losses, searches for these things and intervenes. In the register, in the Ministry of Culture and National Heritage database we currently have 63 thousand. records, i.e. entries concerning such things that we are looking for, about which they were actually proven to be from Poland. As soon as they show up, we intervene immediately. I must say that we meet very often here simply with social assistance. People play detectives themselves a little bit, looking for things like this abroad, in the country. Today, in the age of digitalising the resources of cultural goods very often it is very easy to find a work, which is, for example, more and more easily. in museum warehouses, because warehouses are also digitalized. So, some things that we had no idea before, we didn't know that somewhere in a warehouse, were being revealed today, in a digital age. So that, in my opinion, even in connection with this technological process, in the coming years, there will be an acceleration in the recovery of cultural goods, especially those from World War II. Thank you very much.

This is an area of economic activity which also requires special care. With regard to today's experience of the Environmental Protection Inspection, we must say that even when an inspection of such a person who commits an environmental offence, he will stop, even when the prosecution stops it, it is very often that the courts are maneuvering in such a way that they suspend the decision of the Environmental Protection Inspection so that they do not act to the detriment of entrepreneurs. And just as driving without a driver's license is prohibited, so can the court's decision to suspend proceedings and further such illegal practices may take place. This is, of course, a systemic problem, but I believe that there will also be courage in this Chamber to work together in this area to resolve this problem. As far as the work we are doing at the Ministry of Environment is concerned, work is underway on the proposal to amend the Waste Act. Our Ministry's actions are aimed at improving enforcement efficiency both by control authorities and those which issue administrative decisions on waste management. The proposed amendments to the Waste Act include: the introduction of waste records only via an electronic database on products and packaging and on waste management, in short BDO. It is also planned to abandon the aggregate waste transfer cards. There were situations where somebody said he only checked out once a month, so he could say it was a collective card, right? Even such excuses appeared during inspections or inspections. The changes that we will be able to achieve through the BDO will enable ongoing online control, as I mentioned, not periodic, as has been the case so far. This is to verify the data on waste transfers with the data contained in the waste transfer sheet set up in the database. We'll cross that information to be sure. This instrument will be able to be used both during the control of specific transport and remotely by the competent authorities. He will also allow us to reduce whether to identify the risks or those that are taking action. This system will also generate such reports. In the case of municipal waste management, changes are prepared to seal the waste collection and marketing system by introducing a system allowing for each confirmation of the acceptance of such waste by authorised entities and the settlement of the mass of waste collected from the sites of individual municipalities on a daily basis by the so-called. municipal waste transfer card. Such a form of drawing up of documents used for the purposes of waste records integrated with BDO will also allow the authorities of the Environmental Protection Inspections to have current access to documentation confirmed by municipal waste collectors. The Ministry of the Environment is considering proposing further legislative amendments aimed at sealing existing legislation and facilitating supervision and control of waste management activities. The introduction of a financial guarantee mechanism for waste management entrepreneurs is considered in order to prevent waste from being dumped by their owners. The aim of this solution is to secure the financial resources for the replacement of enforcement procedures for the disposal and management of abandoned waste. This is a flagship project. I really want this bail to be the tool, because the transport of waste is not the transport of substances that are environmentally neutral. It is obvious, but it must also find its reflection in the law. Here's the form of bail. In many areas, many communities demand such a deposit. It's rather the other way around here, but we're going to be very consistent about putting in bail or that kind of financial security. I would also like to say that in some cases the application for authorisation of waste management was made by entities whose financial or property situation was not sufficient to protect against the negative effects of their activities. Here too, as in the case of VAT, there are poles that get permission. Then we find abandoned waste in the barn, and the municipality has to solve this problem. We are also considering a change in the environmental inspection process, and it is these 7 days that we do not need such a notification. This change is necessary because the need for prior notification of the upcoming inspection often prevents irregularities from being detected and hinders the operation of the Environmental Protection Inspection. Of course, today an inspection may enter in certain cases, but dishonest entrepreneurs then use it to complain to inspection that it violates their good name. These kinds of legal tricks are being used, and the courts look at it this way, not different, but this is already a systemic problem for another debate. Environmental Resort also analyzes ongoing cases [[♪ The bell ♪]] infringements of waste management rules which may result in further amendments to the legislation in the near future. Speaking again on the question of measures: it is crucial that, as long as there is an entity responsible for these waste, he is responsible for its disposal. The Environmental Fund under the Land Protection Programme has such measures, but let us bear in mind that it is not a matter of leading to a situation where dishonest entrepreneurs at the expense of the state optimize their production. As they optimized in the case of VAT, they optimize in the case of waste or want to optimize. It is also important that these measures, which are in the framework of the replacement, be on the side of local governments. In cooperation with the voivodship, of course. Thank you.

These first allowances, aimed at restoring the viability of their homes, intended to provide access to drinking water, food, medicines or basic household appliances, are up to 6 000 allowances. zł, that is what the Member asks. The question simply formulated as to how to account for them is the answer, which we have given many times, that these benefits do not require invoices or bills. This is the first aid provided by the State through voivodships and municipalities, namely social assistance centres, which make appropriate decisions. There's no basis here, there's no reason to demand receipts, invoices. This is the first aid for those who have been affected by misfortune. It is different for these higher benefits, which can reach 200,000, for the renovation and reconstruction of damaged houses or up to 100,000, for the renovation or reconstruction of economic buildings for the basic needs of families. There are required settlements, because these are already larger amounts, so bills, invoices. The way in which these special-purpose allowances are to be settled should be defined, it shall be determined, in the decisions which they issue under the authority of the executive bodies of the management board of the social assistance centres. These individual decisions should include the information he is asking about these larger amounts. Allowances for reconstruction and renovation are to be used because they are special allowances intended for a specific purpose, they must be spent according to their intended purpose. If this is a repair and reconstruction allowance, only such measures can be financed from these amounts. They can't be used for other purposes. So here's the fact that the authorities granting these benefits require settlement is understandable and obvious. It is different, I repeat once again, in the case of benefits up to 6,000. zł. If it has happened that, for this reason, the honourable Member is asking, there have been delays in the transfer of special benefits, it is not good. This cannot be understood and accepted. It's irrational in such a case, I would say, the decisions of individual municipalities. I would like to say that all the resources that the voivods have requested have been, and in a very urgent manner, immediately, you can say, put at the disposal of voivodships and local governments. So far, these three voivodships' applications concerned the amount of over PLN 104 million for all the special benefits I mention, and they were immediately mobilised. The highest number of applications were for up to 6 000 allowances. zł, more than 9 thousand. These larger benefits are still under way, because they require preparation, they require the preparation of the front of repair, investment, and this takes longer. But of course. ParlaMint-PL_2017-09-28-sejm-48-2.u74	Thank you. Minister, please respond.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-09-28-sejm-48-2.u75	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! (PL) Mr President, Mr President, ladies and gentlemen, Indeed, the problem that you have raised is an important problem and needs to be addressed. In view of the creation of uniform conditions for persons engaged in human transport brokering activities, acting also under the influence of many signals, opinions which have continued to affect and continue to affect the Ministry, on the need for new regulations in this area, the Ministry of Infrastructure and Construction has undertaken legislative work to amend the rules in the area of passenger transport by passenger cars. The main objective of the draft law on the amendment of the collective transport law and the Act on the working time of drivers is to regulate the functioning of the market for the carriage of persons based on equal, fair rules for all transport market participants. An important element of this work is also the increase in the safety of passengers using services contracted by intermediaries and the introduction of mechanisms for more effective control of the regularity of their business activities by intermediaries and co-operating entities. The project presented by the Ministry of Infrastructure and Construction, which is still in the process of work within the government, provides, among others, for the project. the introduction of a definition of intermediary for the carriage of persons, the establishment of a licence for the transfer of persons, the obligation to commission transport only to the licensed entities referred to in the Road Transport Act of 6 September 2001 and, on the other hand, to facilitate the application by the trader to obtain a licence for the carriage of passengers by passenger cars consisting, inter alia, of a national road transport licence. the abolition of financial requirements as a financial security for such activities, the abolition of compulsory training from local topography and knowledge of local law for applicants for a taxi licence and a number of other changes to ensure equal opportunities, equal rules, equal access to the market for all carriers, to all those who carry out transport, i.e. taxi drivers, but also to all intermediate companies. In our solutions we do not infiltrate the communication between the ordering party, between the client and the intermediary company, nor do we infiltrate the relationship between the intermediary company and the entities, the persons who carry out these orders. We just want the taxi market to have a level playing field for all economic operators, as well as for all economic operators, so that the burden associated with it and the requirements that are imposed are uniform. Thus, the draft law provides for the obligation for intermediaries to keep and make available, for inspection purposes, a register of all persons received and transferred for the execution of orders for the carriage of passengers by road at the request of the Road Transport Inspection and the National Tax Administration. The keeping of a register of orders and a register of employed and collaborating entities may also be useful in the event of a complaint by a passenger concerning possible irregularities in the execution of the ordered carriage. There is a procedure for unfair traders operating in breach of the rules in force which will prevent the use of means of communication for which traffic will be outsourced contrary to the provisions of the Road Transport Act. It is assumed that this procedure can be initiated after an inspection has been carried out with the intermediary, whereby the inspection services will determine the activity of the intermediary without the required licence. It should be stressed that the draft law does not restrict the provision of transport services, but merely indicates that passenger transport can only be carried out by licensed carriers. The intention of the Ministry of Infrastructure and Construction is for all transport market participants to have equal rights and obligations, and none of the groups operating on this market is privileged e.g. by not paying taxes or doing business without registration. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-09-28-sejm-48-2.u82	Thank you very much. And the Minister, please respond.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-09-28-sejm-48-2.u83	Mr. Marshal! Mrs. Member! High Seym! It could be said that the Member has already characterised the system and has described issues related to referrals to spa medicine. It may also be worth adding some information in this regard, and then I will answer the question. The rules for directing patients to spa treatments are laid down in the Regulation of the Minister of Health of 7 July 2011. On the basis of this regulation, the health insurance doctor makes a referral to spa treatment or rehabilitation, taking into account indications, contraindications to spa treatment, which are also defined under the Act on spa medicine. Upon receipt of a referral from a department of the National Health Fund, the relevant cell in the field of health care shall register it, and then after that registration a specialist, a doctor in the field of balneology and physical medicine or medical rehabilitation, shall approve the referral in terms of the purpose of health treatment or health rehabilitation. Then, in accordance with the regulation I referred to, the voivodship department of the National Health Fund confirms the referral if a specialist doctor has approved the purpose of referral and in appropriate health care or health care facilities are available in contracts with these establishments. So someone who receives a referral has the option to leave not only when the purpose of the treatment has been confirmed, but also when there are available seats in these facilities. Now I will answer directly the question of why there are different queues, as you have noticed, depending on the branch. There are three main reasons for these different waiting times and different de facto queue lengths. Firstly, in each voivodship department there are various funds dedicated to spa medicine. Secondly, there are different numbers of places contracted by each branch. And another, there's a different demand in the different departments, so there's a different number of patients in this department who have submitted referral to health care and are waiting for its implementation. The longest waiting times relate to health care services for adults. The number of patients will be targeted at spa treatments depends on the number of places in spa areas that have been contracted by individual branches in a given calendar year, as well as, that is an important element, on the number of people who have resigned from spa treatment, which is related to a given period, as well as to the place of spa treatment. Such a process is very common because patients do not receive treatment at a given time or place. In accordance with the regulation of the Minister of Health of 7 July 2011 on referral to spa medicine, referrals to spa treatment are confirmed according to the order of waiting patients taking into account the scope of benefits and the medical direction, as well as the fact that in the case of adults, the recommended frequency of use of spa treatment is not more than once every 18 months, and this has already been indicated by the Member in her question. So these are the elements that make the time for access to health care services different. In 2015, the financing of health care benefits amounted to about PLN 612 million on the scale of the National Health Fund as a whole, and in the 2017 plan the amount of PLN 659 million was allocated to this range of health care benefits, which means an increase of about 7.6% over 2 years. However, due to the high interest in spa treatment and limited means of queue waiting for spa treatment, it is constantly increasing. The reason for the growing queue is the growing interest in this form of treatment. It should also be noted that for patients who would like to benefit from health care at a convenient time and place, there is also a medical outpatient treatment, which also takes place on the basis of a referral to health care, and the patient himself sets a time limit for treatment with the provider of benefits under a contract with the National Health Fund. This form is less used and offers the opportunity and opportunity to benefit from health care.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-09-28-sejm-48-2.u99	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! To answer your question, I can start by giving some figures and statistics on the status of the Guaranteed Occupational Benefits Fund, including for 2015, 2016 and partly 2017 as well as plans for 2018. The Guaranteed Occupational Benefits Fund implements the provisions of three laws: the Law on special solutions related to job protection, the Act on the protection of employees' claims in the event of insolvency of employers and the Act on the functioning of hard coal mining. If we looked at the state of execution of the fund, the status of the guaranteed benefit fund at the level of 2016, so as not to go any further, at the beginning of 2016 the state of the fund looked so that there was PLN 4.5 billion in the account. Last year it was a little less, which means the state of the fund is improving from year to year. If we look at the budgetary issue, the state of the fund at the beginning of 2018, as we anticipate, will be approaching to the level of PLN 5 billion, PLN 4896 million. These are cash, claims of almost PLN 1 billion, liabilities. The revenue of the fund in 2018 is estimated at 586 million. These are contributions from employers, and I'll remind you that the premium is 0.1%. the basis of dimension, bank interest. Now it's like the other part, the costs of the fund's tasks. We have planned costs at the level of PLN 2308 million, of which more or the largest amount, more than 2 billion, that is exactly what you are asking about, i.e. costs related to the payment of benefits and pre-retirement benefits. The own costs and costs associated with transfers for the implementation of these laws are already the remainder. After all these operations, the fund's status at the end of 2018 will be over PLN 3 billion, which means there is no risk in the operation of the fund, because it is very good. Therefore, our decision to amend the financing rules annually in relation to issues relating to the Labour Fund, i.e. pre-retirement benefits, to transfer this from the Labour Fund to the Guaranteed Occupational Benefits Fund. As far as issues relating to the legal basis are concerned, the inter-ministerial consultation is currently a draft around budget law, in which there is an appropriate provision for the transfer of these funds, but also an additional provision to entrust these tasks to the Guaranteed Occupational Benefits Fund, i.e. all these rules will be maintained. Of course, we also take into account the situation that I was talking about, that the fund's situation is good. It is possible to discuss whether the Labour Fund or the Guaranteed Occupational Benefits Fund is better or worse. These are solutions that we have been implementing for many years, many times on this subject we have discussed whether it would be better for benefits and pre-retirement benefits to be implemented directly from the state budget. But we have the state we have. These funds have been paid from the Labour Fund for many years. It seems that a better place is the Guaranteed Workers' Benefits Fund than the Labour Fund, because we want labour fund funds to be spent on active forms of combating unemployment, including in such a way as completing the programme for young people under the age of 30, as well as co-financing projects that are carried out under the European Social Fund. These are the targets. Here we have the funds provided, there is no threat to the functioning of this. And it is also worth recalling that not long ago we have amended the Act on the Protection of Employee Claims in the event of the employer's insolvency, which will somehow facilitate the use of this law, among others. We extended the reference periods between the termination of the employment relationship and the date of insolvency from 9 to 12 months, extended the catalogue of payments for unmet employee benefits to the equivalent of unused leave, made it possible to pay advances on benefits when the activity ceased. There are still some changes that we have implemented that have entered into force, which are somehow intended to facilitate the use of the Act on the Protection of Employee Claims. If we look at absolute values, the number of people who benefit from this law is definitely decreasing due to the good situation on the labour market. These are connected vessels. And as I said at the beginning, there is at the moment no threat to the functioning of either the Labour Fund or the Guaranteed Occupational Benefits Fund, hence in the Around Budget Act it is a transfer, as well as an appropriate provision allowing the use of these funds from the Guaranteed Occupational Benefits Fund. Thank you.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-09-28-sejm-48-2.u108	Thank you, sir. Please respond to the Undersecretary of State at the Ministry of Development of Mr Tadeusz Kościński. You're welcome.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-09-28-sejm-48-2.u109	Dear Marshal, Ladies and gentlemen! Mr. Messengers! Gentlemen! High Izbo! Special economic zones were created more than 20 years ago. During this time they were not adapted to the needs and economic challenges of Poland. As rightly indicated in the Responsible Development Strategy, the most important task in the area of investment policy is to stimulate high-quality investment that will benefit economic development and ensure stable, highly paid jobs. That's the rule: first of all, an investor, but an even more important employee. The new concept will provide for the possibility of receiving an income tax exemption for new investments. Unlike the existing system, the entrepreneur will be able to seek relief in any area where business can be carried out. A long-term process of changes in the borders of special economic zones will thus be abolished. However, we are not introducing additional bureaucracy. The number of zones and companies managing them will not increase. The companies will support investors in assigned areas and carry out additional tasks related to the responsibility for the investment attractiveness of the assigned area. We will eliminate a situation in which some municipalities, or even districts, were not covered by the zones, and other counties had several zones operating in their territory. After this change, Poland will become more accessible to investors and the decision to grant the tax credit will be issued efficiently and quickly, up to a maximum of one month. Another novelty will be that the investment will be assessed in two areas. Firstly, we will assess it through the level of investment. The investment threshold will be different depending on the size of the company and the level of development of the region. Secondly, we will check whether the investment meets the so-called. quality criteria, which are largely linked to the economic situation of Poland, and take into account the priorities of Poland's economic policy. One of the most important benefits of the new law is to ensure stable conditions for professional development for workers. We rely on the quality of jobs. We will support workers, expecting entrepreneurs to be more involved in improving working and development conditions for current and potential employees. We will implement this, among others. by providing additional benefits to employees and supporting the acquisition of education and qualifications. We will also expect investment to involve a stable employment of staff with specialist education and a wage above the local average wage level in the industry. In response to the needs of our country, we intend to attract high-quality investments that will stimulate local and national development. These investments, which guarantee, among others, will be supported. transfer of know-how, carrying out activities: research and development and development of clusters. We want the products and services produced to be competitive on a regional and global scale. Such investments translate into real economic growth in our country. They are also potential partners for Polish micro, small and medium-sized entrepreneurs who will be able to become co-operatives and suppliers. Sustainable development is also extremely important. Thanks to the changes introduced, we assume that the inflow of investment will be more even on the national scale. This will be served by differentiated criteria, depending on the level of development of the voivodship and unemployment in the district. The higher the unemployment rate in the county compared to the average unemployment in the country, the lower the investment requirements. We also support small and medium-sized enterprises, especially in less developed regions. By opening up the possibility of obtaining support for micro, small and medium-sized enterprises, we assume a greater inflow of investment in less developed areas, which at this stage of development are unable to meet the excessive demands of large investors, such as. infrastructure or the availability of human resources. Investment in medium-sized cities, combining economic and social functions dedicated to a separate government project, will be particularly rewarded. In addition, other medium-sized cities, small towns, urban-rural municipalities and rural communities will also receive preferences. We estimate that as a result of these changes Poland will improve its competitiveness in relation to other countries of the region, because often their offer was better mainly in the area where investments could be made. Thank you.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-09-28-sejm-48-2.u183	Thank you very much for the very interesting questions. Currently, the most important task is to stimulate high-quality investments that will benefit economic development. We consider such investments to be those which, for example, stimulate local entrepreneurship, transfer know-how to Poland, ensure close cooperation with science centres and schools. When preparing the changes, we focused on high quality handling and simple processes, in accordance with the principle: first, the investor and even more important employee. We also care about improving investment attractiveness for other countries. We started to achieve our goals by identifying inefficient processes and rapid autonomy. The priority was to consider pending requests for the integration of investment areas into the boundaries of special economic zones. Investors in some cases waited up to 20 months for decisions. What are the principles of the law and the benefits? The tax exemption instrument is available for new investments, which according to Polish legislation and the definition of the European Commission include the creation of a new production plant and reinvestment. This applies to investments meeting the quantitative criteria, i.e. investment expenditure, and quality criteria, in accordance with the SOR assessed in areas. Sustainable development - investment in high unemployment areas, support for micro, small and medium-sized enterprises, support for medium-sized and small cities and rural areas. Structural development - specialised, highly paid jobs, stable jobs, i.e. employment contract, investment in industries according to the current development policy of the country in which Poland can gain a competitive advantage, export of products or services. Scientific development - development of sectoral clusters, cooperation with universities and research centres in the field of research, R &amp; D activities, employment alongside production workers of active workers. Development of human resources - support for workers in the acquisition of vocational education and qualifications, employee care activities, e.g. participation in additional health care programmes, cooperation with industry schools, creation of patronage classes and transfer of specialist equipment, admission of pupils to practice. The maximum investment project can receive 10 points for compliance with the Responsible Development Strategy. The standard investment must demonstrate compliance with the quality criteria of at least 60%. For investments located in areas with a public aid intensity of 35%, i.e. in the Kujawsko-Pomorskie, Lubuskie, Łódź, Małopolskie, Opole, Pomorskie, Świętokrzyskie and Zachodniopomorskie voivodships, and in the areas belonging to the Ciechanów-Płocki subregions, the Ostrołęcko-Siedlecki, Radom and Warsaw-Eastern threshold decreases from 60% to 50%. For investments located in areas with public aid intensity 50% , such as Lubelskie, Podkarpackie, Warmia-Mazurskie and Podlaskie voivodships, the threshold decreases from 60% to 40%. Investment in medium-sized cities, dedicated to the strategic project "Strategy for Responsible Development', will be particularly promoted within the framework of quality criteria. Furthermore, it is worth noting that medium-sized cities losing their economic and social functions are covered by an additional, separate programme for the regeneration of medium-sized cities. In addition, preference will also be given to other medium-sized, small, urban-rural municipalities and rural communities if the unemployment rate in them is equal to or greater than 160% of the national average. What benefits do we expect? Main benefits of the new law: First of all, the whole of Poland is a special economic zone, a complete departure from territorial restrictions on the adaptation of the instrument to tax exemptions, while respecting the rules concerning the destination of the land. The territorial availability of the instrument will remain dependent only on the destination of the site. This will eliminate the current territorial restriction - a maximum of 25 000. ha of the country's surface, i.e. 0.08% , a long-term procedure for changing the boundaries of special economic zones for an entrepreneur. The handling process will become simpler and will not restrict investors territorially. Secondly, setting the period of tax exemption, introducing a transparent rule - a decision to exempt a fixed-term tax, as a standard, from 10 to 15 years. The higher the intensity of public aid in the voivodship is allowed by the European Union, the longer the exemption period. At the same time, until the expiry of the current Act on Special Economic Zones by the end of 2026 - an additional 5-year preference for areas currently covered by the status and unchanged condition for existing investments. Thirdly, strengthening the role of management companies in the investor service system. Managing companies will become the main point of contact in the region within the one-stop-shop investor service system, i.e. one window in which investors receive comprehensive information regarding the entire investment process and the dedicated employee. Project manager will carry out the entire investment process, will support also at the post-investment stage and will be a regional coordinator of public aid in the area of the tax exemption and government grant instrument. They will also become a centre of knowledge for entrepreneurs about all aspects of economic activity in the region and will continue to specialise in sectors and sectors where they have a competence advantage. At the same time, companies will also be obliged to increase the investment attractiveness of the region. The development of the measures necessary to improve the attractiveness of the area companies will take place in close cooperation with JST companies and representatives of local entrepreneurs and various segments and industries. The companies will operate in the district areas defined by the regulation, which means the end of the price competition period between the companies used by investors to reduce investment costs. Fourthly, action for sustainable development: the differentiation of criteria supports both areas requiring development support and entrepreneurs to step up investment activities. Fifth, unemployment. The higher the unemployment rate in the county compared to the average unemployment in the country, the higher the investment requirements. Micro, small and medium-sized enterprises as well as R &amp; D and business shared services investment projects, criteria tailored to the company's capabilities and the specifics of R &amp; D and business shared services projects, reduced to the standard criteria for large companies carrying out industrial projects - 80% for medium-sized companies respectively, 95% for small companies and investment research and development and business shared services, as well as 98% for micro. These reductions are complementary, which means, for example, that the criterion for a micro-company in a county with the highest unemployment rate is 200,000. Polish zlotys for investment expenditures, open the possibility of obtaining support by micro, small and medium-sized entrepreneurs. We assume a greater inflow of investment in the less developed region. Here, at this stage of development, they are unable to meet the excessive demands of large investors, such as: infrastructure or access to human resources. The bill, as we expect, will be handed over to the interministerial consultation within two weeks. All questions will be answered in writing. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

We proceed with the examination of item 15 of the agenda: Report of the Committee on Public Finance on the Government's draft law amending the Law on Financial Market Supervision and the Act on Insurance and Insurance Activities (prints No. 1782 and 1850). ParlaMint-PL_2017-09-28-sejm-48-2.u190	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The Public Finance Commission at its meeting on 27 September this year considered a government bill amending the Law on Financial Market Supervision and the Act on Insurance and Insurance Activities. The purpose of this draft law is to implement Poland's obligations under the Regulation of the European Parliament and of the Council of 26 November 2014 on key information documents on retail collective investment products and insurance investment products. The Act identifies the Financial Supervision Commission as the authority responsible for the supervision, compliance and enforcement of the provisions of that Regulation of the European Parliament and of the Council of the European Union and imposing administrative penalties for non-compliance with that Regulation. The provisions of this Regulation are mainly technical in nature, impose specific requirements on the creators of collective investment products and insurance investment products or on persons selling such products for the production of documents containing key information on those products. The EU regulation is of general scope and directly applicable in all Member States, but also introduces regulations requiring implementation in the national legal order, which is done in the proposed amendment of the law. In Article 2, paragraph 2 is replaced by the following: Article 1 of the Law of 21 July 2006 is introduced. 3b, which identifies the Financial Supervision Commission as the competent authority within the meaning of the Union Regulation. Art. 3c entitles the administrative penalties referred to in the Regulation to be imposed by decision. In Article 2, paragraph 2 is replaced by the following: Paragraph 2 of the draft law shall be repealed. 1 of the Act of 11 September 2015 on Insurance and Insurance Activities, and paragraph 1. 2 is replaced by the following: Art. 3 sets the date of entry into force of the Act as at 1 January 2018. The original date of application of this Regulation of the European Parliament and of the Council of the European Union was 31 December 2016. However, as a result of the rejection by the European Parliament on 14 September 2016 of regulatory technical standards specifying the elements of the key information document, the European Commission postponed its application until 1 January 2018. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The draft law is compatible with European Union law. On behalf of the Committee on Public Finance, I recommend that the High House adopt this draft Print Act No 1782. Thank you very much.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-09-28-sejm-48-2.u191	Thank you, sir. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. I'm opening a discussion. Mrs Maria Zuba, Parliamentary Club Law and Justice, will be the first to speak. You're welcome.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-09-28-sejm-48-2.u192	Thank you very much. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! As my previous speaker emphasized here, the purpose of introducing the provisions of this law into the Polish legal order is to increase the level of customer protection and meet the requirements of the regulation of the European Parliament and of the Council of the European Union. The regulations in this draft lay down the rules for drawing up key information documents on retail collective investment products and insurance investment products. On the other hand, as the authority responsible for supervision, compliance with and enforcement of EU rules of the Regulation, which was implemented in Polish law, and imposing administrative penalties for non-compliance with the provisions of this Regulation, the government has designated the Financial Supervision Commission. In order to increase the degree of customer protection due to the fact that in the European Union there is a ban on the systematic control of the conditions of insurance products by supervisory authorities, the government in this Act introduces a provision obliging the insurance undertaking carrying out its activities in the territory of the Republic of Poland to forward to the committee a key information document at least 30 days before the start of proposing such a product to the customer and at least 14 days before the start of proposing an update of the functioning key information document. It's such a good security tool. It's possible to react. It is possible to evaluate and correct these documents. The members of the Committee on Public Finance have maintained the deadline proposed by the Government for the entry into force of the provisions of this Law as set out on 1 January 2018, although there was a proposal for 2016. However, the changes in Polish law have made this deadline optimal, and the Members of the Committee on Public Finance accept this term as the one to be respected in the implementation of these provisions. On behalf of the Law and Justice Club, I request that this law be adopted in the form proposed by the Government of Law and Justice and adopted by the Public Finance Commission. Thank you. The project generally implements European Union law. The implementation concerns Regulation No 1286/2014 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 26 November 2014 on documents relating to retail collective investment products and insurance investment products as amended by Regulation No 2016/2340 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 14 December 2016. The Regulation is directly applicable in all Member States. The draft implementing the Regulation amends two laws. The first amendment concerns the Law of 21 July 2006 on the supervision of the financial market. In this respect, the draft law strengthens the role of the Financial Supervision Commission, indicates that the Financial Supervision Commission is responsible for the supervision, compliance and enforcement of the Regulation, including the imposition of administrative penalties by the Financial Supervision Commission for failure to comply with the provisions of that Regulation. The second law affected by this project is the Act of 11 September 2015 on insurance and reinsurance activities. The proposal requires the application of the provisions of Regulation 1286/2014 to the whole sector or insurance system. It introduces quite detailed provisions concerning the functioning of insurance undertakings, insurance agents, banks offering insurance investment products, insurance brokers and requirements for them. The project was subject to numerous consultations with the institutions concerned. In conclusion, it should be noted that the project implementing this EU law increases the level of protection of Polish insurance clients, increases their safety and, I hope, increases citizens' confidence in the activities of insurance companies and other distributors offering insurance investment products. The Parliamentary Club of the Polish People's Party will vote in favour of this project. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-09-28-sejm-48-2.u204	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On 21 June this year. At the meeting of the Committee on Infrastructure, the document was considered: the road safety status and the activities carried out in this respect in 2016 and contained in print No 1547. This document was created at the Secretariat of the National Road Safety Council - which, as I will remind you, is located in the Ministry of Infrastructure and Construction - as a result of the implementation of the statutory obligation imposed by the Road Traffic Law. It's an art. The Commission shall adopt implementing acts laying down detailed In accordance with this Article, the Chairman of the National Road Safety Council shall report annually to the Council of Ministers by the end of March on the state of road safety. The Prime Minister shall then submit such information to the Sejm and the Senate by the end of April each year. The National Road Safety Council report contains, first, information on the state of road safety for a given year, second, the characteristics of the most common types of road events, third, the assessment of the situation in Poland and in each voivodeship and, fourth, the report on the implementation of the implementation programme for the years 2015. The state of security in voivodships is presented in this report in a separate annex. As in previous years reports, so as to be able to compare the situation in individual voivodships with regard to road safety, the so-called. voivodship cards, which identify the main problems that occur in the voivodship concerned, and draw conclusions. Compared to similar reports from previous years, the main part of the document devoted much more space to the presentation of the actions that were undertaken by the government in 2016 and to the presentation of proposals. The statistics on road accidents presented in this report show that 25,500 people died on the roads of the European Union in 2016, i.e. about 600 people more than in 2015, of which in Poland in 33 664 road accidents - by the way: 697 accidents more than in the previous year - 3026 people died. Unfortunately, our country has also seen an increase in the number of people killed, which increased by 3% compared to the previous year, which translates into 88 lives. The average level of threat in the European Union reached 5 deaths per 100,000 in 2016. residents. I am talking about the indicator here, but unfortunately in Poland this indicator is still higher and stands at 7.9 people killed out of 100,000. Citizens, which places us on road safety issues, on the 4th place from the end of the European Union. The number of injured, including severe, also increased and amounted to a total of 40,766 people in 2016. It is 980 people more than in 2015. This indicator here has increased by 2.5%. When it comes to seriously injured people, it was 12 109 people and we have a year-on-year increase, an increase of 909 people, i.e. an indicator of about 8.1%. There was also a certain increase in traffic collisions. According to police records, there were 406,622. Since 2013, the National Road Safety Programme 2013-2020 has been in force in Poland. Unfortunately, the intermediate targets that were set in 2016 were not achieved and the number of deaths was exceeded. This also applies to statistics that apply to severely injured persons. The report shows that the characteristic problem of road traffic in Poland is a very high risk of vulnerable participants, especially pedestrians. Although there has been a 5% decrease in pedestrian fatalities, the number of severely injured pedestrians has increased by 4%. Very many deaths also result in frontal and lateral collisions, the most common causes of which are not adapting the speed to the traffic conditions on the road and not giving priority to the journey. According to the document of the National Road Safety Council, the cost of road accidents in 2015 amounted to about PLN 48 billion, of which almost 34 billion are costs of accidents. Further provision was made for surveillance and road rescue services, etc., etc. The report also provides information on the progress of the implementation of the 2015/2016 implementation programme, which clearly identifies issues related to the need to intensify the tasks envisaged for the implementation of the programme over a period of 2 years. The committee meeting also discussed the state of implementation of the recommendations for the future, which were included in the corresponding report of the National Road Safety Council Secretariat last year. These include, first, the reform of the road safety management system, including formulas, functioning and scope of competence of the National Road Safety Council itself. Secondly, an analysis of the rules that determine the area of road safety and then propose new provisions, including the development of a separate act on road safety. Thirdly, the construction of a system for collecting and making road safety information available, which will improve the implementation of a fact-based policy in this area at every level of management. This is what the finance minister Mateusz Morawiecki proposes, which will increase the amount free in 2018 to an amount of 8 000. zł and will not raise any taxes for Poles. The proposed project lacks solutions that would compensate local governments for lower income tax revenues, there is no compensation for the National Health Fund resulting from a reduction in the amount of health insurance contributions. In my opinion, Kukiz15 has created a project that is impossible to implement, and what is more, it can cause a very serious disturbance in public finances. In the opinion of the National Bank of Poland, the level of losses incurred by the State as a result of the implementation of this parliamentary proposal will exceed 2% GDP. Ladies and gentlemen, 2%. GDP is what Poland will spend on defence in 2018. Such a large amount can also lead in Poland to a deficit of more than 3% GDP and breaches of a stabilising expenditure rule. This project may also increase the debt-to-GDP ratio, which will allow Poland to exceed the 55% threshold set in the Public Finance Act. It seems to me that there is no need for more arguments and reasons for everyone to know and understand that the project is a threat to public finances, and for the sake of the good of Poland it should not be further processed. Circle of Free and Solidarni requests that the bill be rejected and that it not be sent to the committee. Thank you. [[Applause]]

The agreement we are talking about binds the institutions of the Union and the Member States and forms an integral part of the EU legal order. The subject is very important and important, because the transfer and reception of persons residing without authorisation in the territory of countries is necessary to agree, for example, on the current situation relating to immigrants or refugees. This protocol guarantees and ensures good and effective cooperation between both countries in the framework of international action to prevent and combat illegal migration. The work of Poland and Ukraine on this draft protocol and the findings lasted exceptionally long, because almost 10 years. However, the most important thing is that all the ambiguities in the consultation were agreed, which resulted in the signing of the protocol in April this year. The entry into force of this protocol will rationalise and reduce the costs associated with the stay of illegal aliens in special centres. This document is expected, especially by the Border Guard authorities. The conclusion of the protocol will not result in additional costs as well as a reduction of revenue to the state budget, but will also contribute to the deepening of cooperation between the Republic of Poland and Ukraine. In view of the above, Parliamentary Club Law and Justice will vote in favour of this law. Thank you.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-09-28-sejm-48-2.u288	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Kukiz Club15, I have the honour to present the position of the club on the draft Act on the ratification of the Executive Protocol between the Government of the Republic of Poland and the Cabinet of Ministers of Ukraine to the readmission agreement between the European Community and Ukraine, drawn up in Luxembourg on 18 June 2007, signed in Warsaw on 24 April 2017. This bill agrees to the ratification by the President of the Republic of Poland of the Executive Protocol between the Government of the Republic of Poland and the Cabinet of Ministers of Ukraine to the readmission agreement between the European Community and Ukraine. The Agreement concerned shall bind the European Union and its Member States as part of the Union's legal order. It contains e.g. standardise the technical elements related to the transfer and admission of persons residing without authorisation in the territory of both countries, and its approval will ensure effective cooperation between our countries within the framework of international action to prevent and combat illegal migration, which today seems to be a very important element. The protocol sets out, inter alia, the competent authorities to implement the agreement, the border crossing points at which the transit of the readmitted persons will take place, the language of the consultation and such matters. Its adoption should result in closer cooperation between the two countries in the area of legal order protection and prevention and fight against illegal migration. It is a project which is really a necessary element to comply with European Union legislation and international law, which will work between Poland and Ukraine. For these reasons, Kukiz15 declares its support for the project in question. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2017-09-28-sejm-48-2.u289	Thank you very much. And Mr Marek Owa, please.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-09-28-sejm-48-2.u290	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Poselski Club I am proud to present our club's position on the report of the committee on the governmental bill on ratification of the implementing protocol between the Government of the Republic of Poland and the Cabinet of Ministers of Ukraine. The Act authorises, agrees to the ratification of the implementing protocol by the President of the Republic of Poland. The issue of cooperation with the Ukrainian party in the area of readmission has so far been regulated in the Agreement between the Government of the Republic of Poland and the Government of Ukraine on the transfer and admission of persons through the common state border, drawn up in Kiev on 24 May 1993. In view of the fact that the Protocol is an implementing act for the purpose of clarifying the provisions of the Agreement, it should be considered that it takes precedence over the provisions of the 1993 Agreement. In particular, the Protocol sets out the competent authorities entrusted with the task of implementing the Agreement, the border crossing points at which the transfer and transit of readmitted persons will be carried out, and identifies the types of measures used by the parties to send requests and answers, the conditions for the return of persons under escort, the language of communication and consultation and the possibility of working meetings and consultations. The entry into force of the Protocol will not have any negative political impact on both countries, it will also contribute to deepening cooperation between Poland and Ukraine in the area of the broader protection of legal and public order, as well as to increasing the effectiveness of cooperation between the two countries in the field of preventing and combating illegal migration. The Modern Parliamentary Club will vote in favour of this bill. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2017-09-28-sejm-48-2.u291	Thank you. Mr Zbigniew Sosnowski.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-09-28-sejm-48-2.u292	Dear Marshal, Dear Minister, High Izbo! The conclusion of this protocol will ensure effective cooperation between the two states in the framework of international action to prevent and combat illegal migration. In view of the nature and scope of the Protocol, the additional provisions only concern the implementation of readmission and regulate matters which are not covered by the agreement or to which a Member State is not obliged under the agreement. Both the Polish and Ukrainian parties submitted proposals for changes and additions through diplomatic notes from 2008 to 2009. Additional negotiations and arrangements continued until 2016, resulting in the signing of the Protocol in Warsaw on 24 April 2017. The Implementing Protocol sets out the competent authorities responsible for the implementation of the provisions of the Agreement and entitled to cooperate directly. On behalf of the Republic of Poland, the commander of the Main Border Guard was authorised. The Cabinet of Ministers of Ukraine pointed to cooperation with the Polish side of the State Migration Service of Ukraine and the administration of the State Border Service of Ukraine. The Protocol sets out the submission and processing of applications for readmission of own nationals, third-country nationals and stateless persons. The Parties have detailed and listed 11 on the Polish side and five on the Ukrainian side the competent authorities responsible for submitting and examining readmission applications in an accelerated procedure. The eight border crossing points to be used for readmission and transit are indicated in detail, reserving the possibility of possible changes to the list of border crossing points, which is to be agreed by the States Parties in any case. Art. 3 and 4 specify how to apply for readmission, as well as additional measures and documents. The content of the article deserves attention. 6 detailing the confirmation of the transfer of the person. The parties to the agreement on each specific case, including the escort of persons, will inform each individual. Art. 12 treats the official languages in direct communication between the authorities of the parties and in consultation. This means that these are Polish and Ukrainian languages. High Seym! The Polish People's Party does not object to the draft law and will vote in favour of the position of the Committee on Administration and Home Affairs in this matter. Thank you for your attention.

This Protocol was signed in Warsaw on 24 April 2017. There are three reasons why we are strong supporters of this law. Firstly, it is obvious that the Republic of Poland should be with neighbouring countries, and Ukraine is the largest, most populous, most numerous neighbour of the Republic of Poland, not counting the autonomous part of the Kaliningrad region of the Russian Federation... It is clear that we should have an agreement with our neighbours that is up-to-date, precise, accepted by both parties, which would regulate the issue of the return of citizens who have found themselves on both sides of the border in an illegal manner, whether they are nationals of the Ukrainian Republic who are illegally staying in Poland or are illegal immigrants who have entered the territory of the Republic of Ukraine and are not citizens of Ukraine or are indeed stateless persons who have also arrived illegally in Poland. Of course, it also works the other way, i.e. in relation to Polish citizens going from Poland to Ukraine in an illegal way. The second reason why we are in favour of ratifying this protocol is because the contacts between citizens are increasing and not just between the institutions and policies of both countries are getting closer. More and more Ukrainian workers, more and more Ukrainians, students, families of workers or students are staying in the territory of the Republic of Poland, so such an agreement is simply necessary to ensure that the process, which we believe is as positive as possible. In any case, the Polish economy on the work of Ukrainians gains. And there is a third reason why we believe that this protocol should be adopted. Well, in a much more secure way, this protocol provides this path of Ukraine's association with the European Union. We are de facto signing a protocol to an agreement that was concluded between Ukraine and the European Communities. [[♪ The bell ♪]] The purpose of this agreement is the association of our eastern neighbour with the European Communities, and perhaps once a full membership of Ukraine in the European Union. Thank you. [[Applause]]

This Protocol sets out the technical elements relating to the transfer and admission of persons residing without authorisation in the territory of both countries, which are less and less in line with previous legal arrangements. The Protocol shall ensure effective cooperation between the two countries within the framework of international action to prevent and combat illegal migration. According to Art. Article 16 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 of the Agreement, the Republic of Poland as an EU Member State shall be entitled to conclude with Ukraine an implementing protocol to the readmission agreement between the European Community and Ukraine, and the practical implementation of the Agreement shall be governed by the matters referred to in Article. Article 16 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 contract. The issue of cooperation with the Ukrainian party in the implementation of readmission has so far been regulated in the Agreement between the Government of the Republic of Poland and the Government of Ukraine on the transfer and acceptance of persons through the common state border, drawn up in Kiev on 24 May 1993. The entry into force of the Protocol will have no negative political impact on relations between the two countries and will contribute to the deepening of cooperation between the Republic of Poland and Ukraine in the field of protection of the wider legal order. The PO Club will vote in favour of the bill.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-09-28-sejm-48-2.u346	Dear Marshal, High Izbo! On behalf of the Free and Solidarni Circle, I present my position on the proposal for a parliamentary bill amending the Personal Income Tax Act. The contested bill abolishes the limit of PLN 42 764 for the application of 50% the cost of obtaining revenue in relation to the revenue of the creators. Since 2013, creative environments have been paying higher tax costs, among others. for the transfer of ownership of the utility design, industrial design, trade mark or decorative design, as well as copyright and related rights by performers' artists. A group of people covered by this tax are often people who bring many innovations, recognizable in Poland and around the world. In the current legal state, the real rate of PIT for creators on income up to PLN 85 528 per year is 9% , while revenues above this amount are subject to an effective PIT rate of 32%. Such taxation is discriminatory and actually makes the creators partially pay tax on the amount of income rather than income. The amendment proposed in the project is intended to improve the situation of creators and to encourage them to carry out this type of activity. I agree that such a model of change can help to improve the situation of many professionals or scientists. The idea of the project itself to support innovation in Poland is very close to me. Such assumptions are based on the plan of Prime Minister Morawiecki, who repeatedly said that the state must support innovation and the innovative economy. However, it should be stressed that although the project supports development and research activities in Poland by reducing taxes, it does not safeguard other interests of the country. The compensation for the loss of PIT tax to local governments and central budget is very laconic. I am afraid that the entry into force of this project is impossible due to the budget deficit and the budget available to the Minister of Finance. Perhaps the project after the correction and the proposal of solutions for obtaining revenue by local authorities from another source will be possible to adopt. The Free and Solidarni Circle on this bill will cast a holding vote.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-09-28-sejm-48-2.u348	Mrs. Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, Ladies and gentlemen! I have the pleasure as an applicant to present to you print 1751, including a draft law amending the Tax Order, prepared by the Modern Club. The proposal for this revision of the Tax Order is quite simple, and the main objective is to shorten the time limit for reimbursement of the various taxes paid by citizens and, above all, the PIT tax, for which citizens today must wait up to 90 days. We propose that this deadline be shortened to 30 days. We believe that there is no justification for a longer period in the tax system. The current construction of the tax system and the organisation of the tax administration do not really provide incentives for officials and authorities to settle taxes efficiently, including faster reimbursement of the overpaid tax to citizens. The legally accepted tax refund dates come, strangely, from 20 years ago, when offices and officials did not really have support in the form of modern technology and special software. In the vast majority of cases, they had to settle by hand, and so they needed more time. Taxpayers were therefore obliged to wait quite long for the refund of the excess tax and introduced this 90-day deadline. Today, in our view, there is no longer any reason for the government to finance the liquidity of the state at the expense of taxpayers' liquidity, in fact at the expense of citizens' liquidity. Waiting for the refund of overpaid tax also affects taxpayers of excise duty or special hydrocarbon tax, single-member companies of the Treasury and state enterprises, but above all, as I said, every ordinary citizen who is cleared under PIT tax and by the end of April each year is obliged to submit a tax return to the tax office. The head of the tax office, as I said, today has 90 days, counting from the date on which the taxpayer makes this return, which gives us such a deadline that if we have time to make a declaration by the end of April, the tax office has time to pay us the tax refund by the end of July. According to our amendment, every taxpayer would have to be reimbursed by the end of May. Oddly enough, the same, one can say, an additional deadline applies in the case of errors made by the tax office as taxpayers, i.e. if it turns out that we have been waiting for 60 days, the tax office will take into the hand our declaration of annual settlement of the tax on individuals and it will turn out that we have made a mistake there, then a call from the tax office comes to us to explain the error, and the tax office will run again a 90-day deadline for the refund of this tax, which together in the most abstract situations gives the tax office 180 days for the refund of this tax. According to Polish law, the calculation of income tax advances results in the automatic existence of tax overpayments for PIT taxpayers in most cases. For a taxable person who displays only income from a contract of employment in his tax return and does not benefit from any additional tax credit, the amount of that refund shall normally be equal to the amount free of tax, unless the employer takes this amount into account in the meantime in the current accounts. However, if not, this is the amount of up to PLN 1188 in 2017, in previous years it was PLN 556 and 2 gr for each taxpayer. Another situation is that taxpayers with children are expected to pay more refunds. Those benefiting from family relief are more than 4.5 million taxpayers. They get higher amounts of return depending on how many children they have. And they also have to wait 90 days for these refunds, and in extreme cases, as I said, they can wait up to 180 days. Tax reduction solutions, such as tax-free amounts or tax reliefs, aim at reducing the real tax burden and leaving the majority of their really earned wages in the hands of citizens. In practice, then, the later the taxpayer receives a refund of this money, the less benefit for him from this relief, because in the meantime, for example. we may have inflation, which part of the money due to the citizen eats, and in fact the tax office does not pay or charge any interest for this extended period or charge any additional interest. If the taxpayer had paid that money, for example. in a bank account, he would have had a chance to work off inflation and receive interest from the accumulated capital in that account. Like I said, tax reimbursement rules are already 20 years old. Since then, since 1997, significant progress has been made in the computerization of offices, we feel it on a daily basis. Moreover, more and more taxpayers settle PIT tax electronically. In 2015, more than 30% of taxpayers benefited from electronic billing, and this number - it needs to be stressed here - is growing from year to year. The work of the authorities on this basis should therefore be more and more efficient, and the handling of simple, basic citizens' affairs more and more quickly. It can therefore be clearly concluded that this 90-day refund period is not justified. This electronic accounting makes it very easy already completely by reducing to a minimum the duties of the tax office in the context of the tax settlement, in principle limiting them to the verification of the data contained there, entered into the electronic system by the taxpayer. Overpayment of the tax is, as I said, also in the case of other taxpayers, corporate tax, excise duty, so-called. a special hydrocarbon tax. These taxable persons pay tax advances which are often determined on the basis of the profit of the previous year. As you know, year to year can be different - there are fat years, lean years. In the following year, profits may be lower. As a result, companies also sometimes have an excess in the tax paid and such a long period of time for reimbursement also affects their liquidity. There is also a high likelihood of an overpayment in the case of State Treasury companies and state-owned enterprises which are obliged to make monthly advances against profit payments. In a situation where the company does not make a profit of the amount resulting from the advance, there is automatically an excess payment and the extension of the repayment time may, as I said, have a negative impact on the liquidity of the company concerned. In conclusion, this unnecessary delay in passing on tax refunds due to citizens is no longer justified. The computerisation of tax offices over the years has been of great importance - at least it should be important - for the speed of service of citizens and the 90-day deadline for reimbursement of PIT to citizens 20 years ago, but also to other taxpayers I have been talking about should no longer apply. This is the money of our citizens. A citizen who has a chargeable tax to pay must pay it usually within a few days of making a declaration, and the State, using its advantage, without deciding on a partnering style of service for citizens in offices, imposes a very long deadline for reimbursement as unjustified. So much for me. Of course, if there are any questions from you, I would be happy to answer them all. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2017-09-28-sejm-48-2.u349	Thank you very much, Member. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. I'm opening a discussion. And I will ask Mr Bartłomiej Stawiarski to speak on behalf of the Law and Justice Club.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-09-28-sejm-48-2.u350	Dear Marshal! High Izbo!
